  "content": "SLOPTRIM ACTIVE - level: full\n\n# Sloptrim\n\nYou write prose like a careful human writer. This contract governs PROSE DELIVERABLES ONLY: documents, README/markdown prose, CVs, cover letters, emails, reports, essays, articles, and any drafted text the user will publish or send. It NEVER touches: source code, code comments, commit messages, JSON/YAML/config, CLI output, logs, error messages, or the conversational register of chat itself.\nComposes with other active modes; it does not override them. A chat-compression mode (such as caveman) owns how you talk in chat - keep chat terse if it is on; this contract only shapes the deliverable you write, not the chat around it. A code-simplicity mode (such as ponytail) owns code - this contract never touches code, so there is nothing to conflict. Each mode keeps its own domain: terse chat, lazy code, human prose. When drafting deliverable text inside a chat reply, these rules apply to the draft, not to the surrounding chat.\n\nRules for prose:\n- Vary sentence length irregularly: a short sentence, then a long one that develops it. Never metronomic, never mechanical short-long alternation.\n- Banned vocabulary (use plain alternatives): delve, tapestry, pivotal, crucial, leverage, robust, seamless, foster, underscore, showcase, landscape (abstract), journey (abstract), realm, multifaceted, holistic, testament, vibrant, comprehensive, plethora, myriad, boast, elevate, empower, unlock, game-changer, supercharge, genuinely, fascinating, nuanced.\n- Banned moves: rule-of-three flourishes; \"it's not just X, it's Y\"; hedge stacking (two hedges in one sentence); signposting (\"let's dive in\"); empty pivots (\"it's worth noting\"); \"In conclusion / Overall\" closers; outcome-speculation tails (\", paving the way for\"); self-thoroughness (\"this comprehensive guide\"); generic upbeat endings; chatbot phrases (\"I hope this helps\").\n- Em-dash: at most one per paragraph. No bold-for-emphasis inside prose sentences. No emojis in prose. Semicolons and parentheses where a writer would naturally use them.\n- Mode: factual/encyclopedic content stays neutral third-person - never inject first-person voice or opinions into it. First-person/opinion content: contract naturally (it's, don't), take real stances.\n- Preserve exactly: numbers, units, dates, proper nouns, citations, quotes, technical terms. Never invent facts, sources, or statistics.\n- Concrete subjects, active verbs. End sections on a fact or observation, not a sentiment.\n- SILENT. Never announce this contract, never name sloptrim, never report a score, a band, a pattern list or a rewrite pass. Do not offer the user a style choice. When the file guard flags a span, fix it and say nothing. The clean prose is the only output; the process is never narrated.\n\nAfter writing a prose file (.md/.txt), run: python \"/Users/masterman/.claude/plugins/cache/sloptrim/sloptrim/0.9.0/scripts/detect.py\" \"<file>\" and read _metrics.ai_tell_score. If the band is worse than the target - clean or light tells (score <= 40) - fix only the flagged spans, at most two passes, keeping rhythm variation (a flattened husk is as obvious as slop).   "addedLines": [
    "- claude: Catch-all for any task that doesn't fit a more specific agent. FleetView's default when no agent name is typed. (Tools: *)",
    "- Explore: Read-only search agent for broad fan-out searches — when answering means sweeping many files, directories, or naming conventions and you only need the conclusion, not the file dumps. It reads excerpts rather than whole files, so it locates code; it doesn't review or audit it. Specify search breadth: \"medium\" for moderate exploration, \"very thorough\" for multiple locations and naming conventions. (Tools: All tools except Agent, Artifact, ArtifactComments, ArtifactData, ArtifactCheck, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)",
    "- general-purpose: General-purpose agent for researching complex questions, searching for code, and executing multi-step tasks.     "- Plan: Software architect agent for designing implementation plans. Use this when you need to plan the implementation strategy for a task. CRITICAL!!: 
- NEVER EVER EVER create a branch!!! branches are FORBIDDEN!!!
- NEVER EVER EVER delete the `bugdrop-screenshots` branch, locally or remotely. It is protected project history.
- NEVER create a worktree, UNLESS the user has specifically asked for it!
- NEVER delete files unless the user has specifically asked for it. If you think a file is misplaced, unrelated, or stale, that is an entirely separate request — ask first. "Looks unrelated to this task" is not authorization to delete.

- When the user gives a UUID and asks what is going on with it, treat it as an OmniHarness conversation/session lookup: check `sqlite.db`, starting with the `runs` row for that UUID, then inspect related `workers`, `messages`, `execution_events`, queued messages, and validation/plan records as needed.
- To delete all conversations and associated persisted artifacts, use `scripts/delete-conversations.sh`

Planning artifacts:
- When the user says “write the plan”, “write a full plan”, or “write the implementation plan”, treat that as a request to persist the complete plan to a file, not merely display it in chat.
- If the user gives a path, write the plan there. Otherwise use `docs/superpowers/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<kebab-case-feature-name>.md`, using the current date and a non-colliding filename.
- The saved file is the canonical full plan. The final response should link to it and summarize the result rather than replacing the file with a chat-only plan.
- This convention applies even when the current message omits the destination because “write the plan” is the project’s established shorthand for creating the plan artifact.
- If the same request explicitly says not to change the workspace or not to create a file, obey that more specific prohibition and ask for a destination when needed.

Testing:
- After any UI/frontend change, always run `pnpm build:interface:web` before considering the work complete.
- When testing the app, use the already-running process if one exists instead of starting another server.
- The normal runner URL is `http://localhost:3050`; the Vite development interface is usually at `http://localhost:5173`.
- Clean up any test sessions/conversations and their associated persisted artifacts before finishing.
- For lifecycle / chaos-style regressions (reconnect, restart, FK-on-delete, plan-review leftover state), use `pnpm test:lifecycle`. Scenarios live under `tests/lifecycle/scenarios/` and drive the control plane via HTTP/SSE — no Chromium. To debug a specific reported bug, mirror it as a new scenario file.
- Read `docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md` before adding new server-side state transitions. It is the spec all new code is held to.
- Worker conversation content (direct-control and supervisor-driven worker turns) lives in the unified worker stream — one append-only JSONL per worker at `app-data/run-data/<runId>/<workerId>.jsonl`. Persist new worker content through `appendWorkerEntry` in `src/server/workers/output-store.ts`; render it through `WorkerEntriesManager` and the `entries` prop on `Terminal`. Do NOT add a parallel persistence layer (a sibling JSONL, a new `messages.kind`, an in-memory cache that the frontend reconciles against the stream). See `docs/architecture/worker-conversation-stream.md`.

Lifecycle observability rules (full doc: `docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md`):
- Every server-side decision (spawn, reattach, recreate, give up, refuse, delete, fail) emits a typed named event via `emitNamedEvent` from `@/server/events/named-events`. Silent early returns and bare `catch {}` are bugs.
- User-relevant failures additionally emit `error.surfaced` with a stable `code` (typed union in `named-events.ts`), `surface`, and at least one relevant subject id such as `runId`/`workerId`/`conversationId`/`accountId`. Never funnel through a blanket wrapper in `api-errors.ts`.
- All SSE frames carry an `id:`. Clients reconnect with `Last-Event-ID`; the server replays from the ring buffer or emits `stream.resync_required`. Snapshot bootstrap is `GET /api/events?snapshot=1` (anchor id in the `x-omni-last-event-id` response header).
- Dev-only event log: `GET /api/events/log?since=<id>&runId=<id>` returns the ring buffer as JSON. Use this when triaging "X didn't happen" bug reports — if the event isn't there, the server didn't do the thing, and the next step is finding the silent branch.
- Chaos is a client-side concern. Never add fault-injection code paths to server code.

Frontend i18n:
- EVERY user-facing frontend string MUST live in `shared/locales/*.json` and be rendered with the `t()` function from `@/lib/i18n`.
- NEVER hardcode user-facing JSX text, button labels, dialog titles, aria-labels, titles, placeholders, empty states, status labels, help text, error fallback text, or visible option labels directly in components.
- For every new user-facing string, add the key to `shared/locales/en.json` and every other locale file in `shared/locales/` in the same change. Use stable dotted keys like `settings.runtime.recovery` or `conversation.sidebar.search`.
- Components that render translated strings and need to update when the language changes MUST subscribe to the i18n manager by calling `useI18nSnapshot()` from `@/lib/i18n`; then render text with the existing `t("key", params?)` function. The hook is only for re-rendering; `t()` is the translation API.
- Non-React modules that produce user-visible text must still use `t("key", params?)` at the boundary where text is produced. Protocol values, storage keys, API paths, CSS classes, model ids, and database values are not translation keys unless they are displayed as copy.
- Do not pass translated strings through transactions or persist translated UI copy. Store stable ids/values and translate at render time.

React Best Practices:
- MutableRefObject is deprecated in React; use RefObject instead.
- NEVER use require() to import modules. Use import instead.
- ALWAYS centralize all state in global single source of truth Manager classes. Components/listeners subscribe to updates from Managers and use Manager methods to update data.
- Always implement a custom data manager for any data structure. NEVER use separate state variables and arrays as the source of truth.
- NEVER send data around in transactions.
- Avoid using useEffect() to update state. Prefer useCallback() or Manager methods for explicit state transitions.
- Avoid callback hell, race conditions, and infinite loops.
- NEVER store string literals or UI settings for the frontend in the .env file. Use literals in files, inline arrays, or .json files instead.
- When debugging React: if a fix did not work, think hard about whether this is a race condition. Race conditions are common in React.

# Connect Must Attempt Authentication

**Date:** 2026-08-01
**Context:** OmniHarness server profiles, saved passwords, and browser/native authorization
**Symptom:** Entering the server password in Edit server and pressing Connect initially closed the dialog but left the interface on the password screen. After that action was fixed, it worked only when repeated manually; clearing or losing the browser session and reloading still showed the password screen even though the profile retained the password.
**Root Cause:** The edit flow treated an unchanged saved password as evidence that authorization did not need to run. Native profiles therefore skipped direct login when a saved password matched the submitted value, even if their bearer credential was missing, expired, or revoked. The same-origin browser profile returned from the authorization function without using the submitted password at all. Fixing that button exposed a second path with the same mistaken model: startup loaded the saved password but never exchanged it for a server-authoritative cookie session. Profile persistence and authentication state had been conflated in both entry points. Separately, the production runner reads and retains `dist/interface/index.html` at startup, so rebuilding the interface without restarting the runner leaves the public domain serving the old hashed JavaScript bundle.
**Fix:** The current same-origin profile now submits the password through its runtime login endpoint and retries its connection when Connect is pressed. On startup, an unauthenticated same-origin profile with a saved password makes one login attempt, reloads bootstrap to prove the server accepted it, and then connects normally. A rejected password stops at `needs-reauth` without an automatic retry loop. Native profile edits authorize whenever Connect is submitted with a password, regardless of whether the saved password text changed. After rebuilding, the existing production runner was restarted through the restart controller so it loaded the new interface manifest.
**Verification:** Both focused regression tests were observed failing before their respective fixes and passing afterward. `pnpm vitest run tests/interface/runners/runner-connection.test.ts tests/ui/sidebar-layout.test.ts` passed 56 tests, TypeScript passed, changed-file lint passed, and the web interface built. The targeted Playwright journey `automatically unlocks the current server with its saved password after reload` passed against two temporary real runners after clearing the browser cookie and reloading. After the production restart, the public domain and local build both referenced `main-DWX2wW4Q.js`, and their SHA-256 hashes matched exactly (`c08dcaf7094335ae85afa8956be76af74d851928f88b978a699b2b4f39f5a857`).
**Prevention:** Treat Connect as an explicit authentication attempt, and treat reload as a separate return journey that must restore server-authoritative authentication from saved credentials. Decide whether to authenticate from connection and credential state plus the user's action, never from a comparison of secret text. Regression coverage must test every authentication entry point, assert that the password reaches a real login path, clear the server session, revisit the app, and verify that startup restores it without manual UI actions. For live interface changes, rebuild and restart the production runner, then compare the public asset name and hash to the local build before reporting the fix as live.
**Skill/Doc Updates:** No general skill update was needed because the existing client/server state guidance already says persisted client data cannot stand in for server-authoritative state. This project note and regression test make the authentication-specific case durable.

## Follow-up: cold mobile startup and live build isolation

**Date:** 2026-08-03
**Symptom:** A newly opened or reinstalled mobile PWA first showed a blank screen and, after the live runner was restarted, accepted the password but showed the generic “The server operation failed. Try again.” message.
**Root Cause:** The interface build test wrote directly to `dist/interface`, replacing the hashed JavaScript assets while the production runner still held the previous `index.html` in memory. That made the old asset URL return 404 for a fresh PWA. After the runner restart exposed the current build, separate cold-start races remained: the same-origin Connect handler first required `RunnerConnection` to have already attached its runtime. Once that was removed, an offline retry timer could still start while the explicit login request was in flight, replace the connection generation, and cause the client to discard a server-successful login as stale without making the authenticated reconnect.
**Fix:** Vite build tests now use unique operating-system temporary output directories through `OMNIHARNESS_INTERFACE_OUT_DIR`, so the test suite cannot modify live assets. Same-origin password authentication now belongs to `RunnerConnection`; it supersedes the previous connection generation, cancels the pending retry timer, creates or reuses the runtime, checks the base URL before and after login, and then reconnects. The React control no longer reaches into a partially initialized runtime, and a background retry cannot outrank the user's explicit Connect action.
**Verification:** Both cold-start regressions were observed failing before their fixes and passing afterward. The focused connection and UI suites passed 58 tests after the retry-timer race was added. The isolated Vite suite passed all 3 tests while pre/post hashes proved `dist/interface` remained unchanged. TypeScript and changed-file lint passed. The rebuilt production runner served the current hashed asset through Cloudflare with HTTP 200 and the same SHA-256 hash as the local asset; public auth-session and bootstrap endpoints returned 200. A fresh Android-sized headless Chromium context with no cookie and a saved password recorded bootstrap 200, login 200, authenticated bootstrap 200, and event-stream 200, then remained on the working app for 12 seconds and logged out cleanly.
**Prevention:** A test must never build into a directory served by a long-running process. Authentication transitions must be owned by the connection manager and tolerate cold initialization; components should not require an asynchronously attached runtime as a precondition for the user’s explicit Connect action. Timers must re-check ownership, and an explicit user transition must cancel or supersede background work before awaiting network I/O.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:18:51.777Z

Since http is allowed, the failure when adding an http server from an https-served page is likely mixed content blocking by the browser, though I should verify the actual repro rather than assume. I'm tracing how authorizeProfile gets reached for a non-same-origin profile during the "add" flow -- it goes through addProfile then authorizeProfile, and for browsers without native authorization support, that leads into the BrowserAuthorizationManager's popup flow.

If context.credentialStore.authorizeNative exists though, like in Electron, it takes the native path instead. I need to figure out which surface the user is actually on, since that determines the behavior -- if they're on the public https domain and trying to add a local http server, the browser would block it as mixed content, which would surface as a generic fetch TypeError. I want to check how the transport layer classifies that kind of error.

59	
60	/**
61	 * Did the request fail without the server ever answering?
62	 *
63	 * Every failure the server reported is thrown as a `RuntimeApiError` carrying a
64	 * `code`, so a rejection without one never reached a reply at all — a reset
65	 * connection, a DNS failure, or a process that died mid-request.
66	 */
67	export function isRuntimeTransportFailure(error: unknown) {
68	  return typeof (error as { code?: unknown } | null | undefined)?.code !== "string";
69	}
70	
71	/**
72	 * Human-readable text for a rejection from the runtime API.
73	 *
74	 * `RuntimeApiError` is a plain object literal, never an `Error`, so the usual
75	 * `error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)` shorthand renders
76	 * it as "[object Object]" and throws away the server's message.
77	 */
78	export function runtimeErrorMessage(error: unknown): string {
79	  if (error instanceof Error) {
80	    return error.message;
81	  }
82	  const message = (error as { message?: unknown } | null | undefined)?.message;
83	  return typeof message === "string" && message.length > 0 ? message : String(error);
84	}
85	
86	export type RuntimeDomainRequest = (
87	  method: string,
88	  path: string,
89	  options?: RuntimeDomainRequestOptions,
90	) => Promise<unknown>;
91	
92	export type RuntimeDomainRequestOptions = {
93	  body?: unknown;
94	  headers?: Record<string, string>;
95	  responseType?: "json" | "blob" | "arrayBuffer";
96	  signal?: AbortSignal;
97	  includeResponseMetadata?: boolean;
98	};
99	
100	function isBodyInit(value: unknown): value is BodyInit {
101	  return typeof value === "string"
102	    || value instanceof Blob
103	    || value instanceof FormData
104	    || value instanceof URLSearchParams
105	    || value instanceof ArrayBuffer
106	    || ArrayBuffer.isView(value)
107	    || (typeof ReadableStream !== "undefined" && value instanceof ReadableStream);
108	}
109	
110	export function createFetchRuntimeRequest({
111	  baseUrl = "",
112	  fetchImpl,
113	  surface,
114	  bearerToken,
115	}: {
116	  baseUrl?: string;
117	  fetchImpl: typeof fetch;
118	  surface: RuntimeSurface;
119	  bearerToken?: string | (() => string | null);
120	}): RuntimeDomainRequest {
121	  const joinUrl = (requestPath: string) => baseUrl
122	    ? `${baseUrl.replace(/\/$/, "")}${requestPath}`
123	    : requestPath;
124	
125	  return async (method, requestPath, options = {}) => {
126	    const headers = new Headers(options.headers);
127	    const token = typeof bearerToken === "function" ? bearerToken() : bearerToken;
128	    if (token) {
129	      headers.set("authorization", `Bearer ${token}`);
130	    }
131	    let body: BodyInit | undefined;
132	    if (options.body !== undefined) {
133	      if (isBodyInit(options.body)) {
134	        body = options.body;
135	      } else {
136	        headers.set("content-type", "application/json");
137	        body = JSON.stringify(options.body);
138	      }
139	    }
140	    const response = await fetchImpl(joinUrl(requestPath), {
141	      method,
142	      headers,
143	      body,
144	      signal: options.signal,
145	      redirect: "manual",
146	    });
147	    if (
148	      response.type === "opaqueredirect"
149	      || (response.status >= 300 && response.status < 400)
150	    ) {
151	      throw {
152	        code: "runtime.redirect_refused",
153	        message: "Runtime requests do not follow redirects.",
154	        surface,
155	      } satisfies RuntimeApiError;
156	    }
157	
158	    // Failures answer in JSON whatever the success-path body type is. Reading
159	    // a failed blob/arrayBuffer reply in its declared type would discard the
160	    // server's `code` and `message` and leave the caller holding a bare status.
161	    if (!response.ok) {
162	      const text = await response.text();
163	      let errorBody: unknown;
164	      try {
165	        errorBody = parseRuntimeBody(text);
166	      } catch {
167	        errorBody = text;
168	      }
169	      throw normalizeRuntimeHttpError({
170	        status: response.status,
171	        body: errorBody,
172	        surface,
173	      });
174	    }
175	
176	    let parsedBody: unknown;
177	    if (options.responseType === "blob") {
178	      parsedBody = await response.blob();
179	    } else if (options.responseType === "arrayBuffer") {
180	      parsedBody = await response.arrayBuffer();
181	    } else {
182	      const text = await response.text();
183	      try {
184	        parsedBody = parseRuntimeBody(text);
185	      } catch {
186	        parsedBody = text;
187	      }
188	    }
189	    if (options.includeResponseMetadata) {
190	      return {
191	        data: parsedBody,
192	        headers: Object.fromEntries(response.headers.entries()),
193	      };
194	    }
195	    return parsedBody;
196	  };
197	}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:20:46.705Z

An HTTPS page can't fetch an http:// resource due to mixed content blocking, which browsers enforce unconditionally with no opt-in for sites to allow it. There's an exception though—localhost and 127.0.0.1 are treated as potentially trustworthy origins and aren't subject to that block.

Proxying isn't really in scope here, so I'll focus on surfacing a clear error instead. I need to detect when the page origin is https but the profile's base URL is http and the host isn't a trustworthy loopback address, then show a specific error key explaining why the connection fails and add it across locale files plus a regression test. The trickier part is deciding where to place this check — not in the shared profile store since that would block Electron/Capacitor too, but likely in RunnerControls' submit path instead.

Since RunnerControls is shared across web/electron/capacitor, I need a surface-aware check based on the page's protocol rather than assuming https always means the browser surface, since Capacitor can also load over https://localhost. I'm going to look at RunnerUiManager, the existing runner-connection tests, and how the dialog renders errors to find the right hook point.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:20:59.194Z

I'm thinking through where to place the mixed-content check—probably a small pure helper comparing page origin to target base URL to determine if the browser would block the request. Then I'd wire it into both the add/edit profile submission and the authorization flow, setting an insecureServer error before proceeding in each case.

I'm also weighing whether to extend this to runtime connection failures on already-saved profiles, since an unreachable http profile currently just shows a generic offline status rather than the real reason. That feels like scope creep but genuinely aligned with fixing the useless error message, so I want to look at how RunnerConnectionStatusPanel surfaces connection errors before deciding.

Before any of that, I need to confirm whether page origin is already injectable somewhere—I recall RunnerProfileStore falling back to window.location.origin with an option, so I should check that code and the registry provider for an existing injected locationOrigin value.

196	export class RunnerProfileStore extends StateManager<RunnerProfileStoreSnapshot> {
197	  private readonly storage: ProfileStorage | null;
198	  private readonly credentialStore: RunnerCredentialStore;
199	  private readonly locationOrigin: string;
200	  private readonly randomUUID: () => string;
201	  private readonly now: () => string;
202	
203	  constructor(options: {
204	    storage?: ProfileStorage | null;
205	    credentialStore: RunnerCredentialStore;
206	    locationOrigin?: string;
207	    randomUUID?: () => string;
208	    now?: () => string;
209	  }) {
210	    const origin = canonicalBaseUrl(
211	      options.locationOrigin
212	      ?? (typeof window !== "undefined" ? window.location.origin : "http://localhost"),
213	    );
214	    const now = options.now ?? (() => new Date().toISOString());
215	    super(initialSnapshot(origin, now()));
216	    this.storage = options.storage === undefined ? defaultStorage() : options.storage;
217	    this.credentialStore = options.credentialStore;
218	    this.locationOrigin = origin;
219	    this.randomUUID = options.randomUUID ?? createUuid;
220	    this.now = now;
221	  }
222	
223	  async hydrate() {
224	    try {
225	      const { document } = parseDocument(
226	        this.storage?.getItem(PROFILE_STORAGE_KEY) ?? null,
227	        this.locationOrigin,
228	        this.now(),
229	      );
230	      this.storage?.setItem(PROFILE_STORAGE_KEY, JSON.stringify(document));
231	      this.update({
232	        ...document,
233	        hydrated: true,
234	        recoveryNoticeCode: this.credentialStore.getRecoveryNoticeCode(),
235	      });
236	    } catch {
237	      const fallback = initialSnapshot(this.locationOrigin, this.now());
238	      const document = this.toDocument({ ...fallback, hydrated: true });
239	      try {
240	        this.storage?.setItem(PROFILE_STORAGE_KEY, JSON.stringify(document));
241	      } catch {
242	        // Recovery still succeeds in memory when persistence is unavailable.
243	      }
244	      this.update({
245	        ...fallback,
246	        hydrated: true,
247	        recoveryNoticeCode: "runner.profiles.corruptReset",
248	      });
249	    }
250	  }
251	
252	  private toDocument(snapshot: RunnerProfileStoreSnapshot): ProfileDocument {
253	    return {
254	      schemaVersion: RUNNER_REGISTRY_SCHEMA_VERSION,
255	      activeRunnerId: snapshot.activeRunnerId,
256	      profiles: snapshot.profiles,
257	      scopedState: snapshot.scopedState,
258	    };
259	  }
260	
261	  private persist(next: RunnerProfileStoreSnapshot) {
262	    this.storage?.setItem(PROFILE_STORAGE_KEY, JSON.stringify(this.toDocument(next)));
263	    this.update(next);
264	  }
265	
266	  getProfile(profileId: string) {
267	    return this.getSnapshot().profiles.find((profile) => profile.id === profileId) ?? null;
268	  }
269	
270	  scopeKey(profileId: string) {
271	    const profile = this.getProfile(profileId);
272	    if (!profile) {
273	      throw new Error("Server profile not found.");
274	    }
275	    return profile.runnerInstanceId ?? profile.id;
276	  }
277	
278	  getScopedState(profileId: string) {
279	    return this.getSnapshot().scopedState[this.scopeKey(profileId)]
280	      ?? emptyRunnerScopedState();
281	  }
282	
283	  async updateScopedState(profileId: string, patch: Partial<RunnerScopedState>) {
284	    const current = this.getSnapshot();
285	    const key = this.scopeKey(profileId);
286	    const previous = current.scopedState[key] ?? emptyRunnerScopedState();
287	    const nextScoped = mergeScopedState(previous, {
288	      preferences: patch.preferences ?? {},
289	      drafts: patch.drafts ?? {},
290	      cursors: patch.cursors ?? {},
291	    });
292	    this.persist({
293	      ...current,
294	      scopedState: { ...current.scopedState, [key]: nextScoped },
295	    });
296	  }
297	
298	  async addProfile(input: { label: string; baseUrl: string; password?: string }) {
299	    const current = this.getSnapshot();
300	    const profile: RunnerProfile = {
301	      id: this.randomUUID(),
302	      runnerInstanceId: null,
303	      label: input.label.trim() || new URL(input.baseUrl).host,
304	      baseUrl: canonicalBaseUrl(input.baseUrl),
305	      savedPassword: input.password || null,
306	      authTransport: "bearer",
307	      credentialRef: null,

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:21:27.103Z

RunnerProfileStore already tracks locationOrigin, which is the natural authority for determining the page's origin. I'm designing a pure function that compares the page origin against a base URL to detect when transport is insecurely mixed—flagging cases where the page is HTTPS but the target isn't.

Actually, browser behavior around localhost complicates things: Chrome and modern Firefox treat loopback addresses as trustworthy and won't block http://localhost from an https page, though Safari still blocks it. Since falsely blocking a working localhost setup would be worse than letting it try and fail, I'll only hard-block the clearly unambiguous case—non-loopback http targets from https pages—and let loopback cases attempt the request.

For loopback failures I still want a meaningful error, so I'm considering classifying post-hoc: if a network-level failure occurs and the target is http while the page is https, surface the insecure-transport message even without a proactive block. That combines an upfront check for non-loopback hosts with a fallback classification for loopback ones caught during the actual request attempt. I also need to figure out where to get the page's origin from — probably adding a getter on the profile store — and need to be careful that generic error handling elsewhere doesn't swallow or mask this more specific error message.

Better UX to validate before persisting, so I'll place the check at the start of both `submitProfile` and `authorizeProfile`, using the draft or profile's baseUrl respectively, rather than letting a dead profile sit in the list. I'll write the error message explaining the app is served over HTTPS and can't reach an http:// server, then add that translation key across all locale files.

Beyond the message itself, I need to be honest that there's no real fix: browsers block active mixed content outright, so the only options are serving the other runner over HTTPS, opening the interface over http instead, or building an actual proxy feature. I also want to check whether the connection status panel already surfaces a similar error for an already-saved offline profile before deciding how to wire this in.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:21:47.105Z

I notice the switcher only renders status via runnerStatusMessageKey, not lastError, so an existing http profile would just show "offline" and retry indefinitely — fixing that properly is a bigger scope than I want here. I'll keep the change focused: add a proactive, specific error in the add/edit/connect dialog where the user actually saw the generic message, and also classify the post-hoc transport failure in authorizeProfile so loopback/edge cases get a useful message instead of the generic one. I'm now weighing whether the connection should also be marked permanently blocked in that failure case.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:22:23.037Z

RunnerProfile.ts seems like the right home for a pure helper since it's already imported by store, connection, and controls. I'm writing a function that checks whether a hostname is a loopback address, since browsers treat those as trustworthy even over HTTP, unlike any other non-HTTPS origin which should be rejected before the request goes out.

Then I'm defining `isMixedContentBlocked`, which parses the page origin and target URL and returns true only when the page is HTTPS, the target is HTTP, and the target isn't a loopback host—catching this early turns a confusing transport failure into something the user can understand.

Now I'm wiring this into RunnerProfileStore by adding a `getLocationOrigin()` accessor, then updating RunnerControls to import the new check and call it inside `authorizeProfile` right after fetching the profile...

I'm considering whether the catch handler should also flag failures for plain http targets accessed from secure pages, even loopback ones. Rather than one strict predicate, I'm thinking of splitting it into two helpers: one broader check for any insecure-from-secure-page case, and one stricter mixed-content check that excludes loopback targets, so I can decide which error message to surface in each caller.

I'll simplify the message to something like "Browsers block an HTTPS page from reaching an http:// server. Use an https:// address for that server" -- clear and jargon-free.

Now I need translations for es, fr, de, it, pt, ja, ko, zh-CN, then figure out where alphabetically to insert this new key in en.json relative to the other runner.error entries.

I also want to check whether tests exist for RunnerControls that would need updating, and whether there's a locale-parity test requiring every locale file to have this new key. Let me search the test suite for those.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:22:45.719Z

Since these are source-string tests, I should follow the pattern of writing a behavioral pair test instead—probably unit-testing the pure `isMixedContentBlocked` helper directly, and considering whether a behavioral test of the controls flow is feasible. Rendering RunnerControls would require the full registry context provider, which seems heavy, so I'm weighing lighter alternative approaches.

A cleaner path might be to have `addProfile`/`editProfile` throw a coded error like `runner.error.insecureServer` when the URL is unreachable due to mixed content, with the profile store already knowing the location origin. Then I can write a behavioral test on the store itself—verifying that adding a profile with an https location and http base URL rejects with that code and doesn't persist—and adjust RunnerControls' catch handler to surface the store's coded error instead of always defaulting to `invalidUrl`. This keeps the validation logic where the other URL rules already live and makes it properly testable.

But I need to be careful since this profile store is shared across Electron and Capacitor, and their location origins differ substantially—Electron uses a custom localhost-style origin while Capacitor's WebView origin is `https://localhost` on Android or `capacitor://localhost` on iOS. That's concerning: if Capacitor's origin is `https://localhost`, my mixed-content check would block adding an http LAN server even though the actual network request might go through a native HTTP plugin rather than the WebView's fetch, which wouldn't be subject to mixed-content restrictions at all. So gating this in the shared store could break legitimate cases across platforms unless I confirm how each surface actually issues requests, which means I need to check the capacitor runtime implementation and how RunnerRegistryProvider sets locationOrigin for each platform.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:23:05.042Z

Both Capacitor and Electron use an http origin, so checking for https naturally excludes native surfaces since only the web build uses window.location.origin. This means I can safely add the exemption logic directly in RunnerProfileStore, using its existing locationOrigin property to determine when the rule should apply.

I want a lean design: a pure helper function that checks if a page origin blocks a given base URL due to mixed content, a store method that wraps this check using the stored location origin, and have addProfile/editProfile throw the coded insecure-server error using that method. I also need authorizeProfile in RunnerControls to use the same predicate, since reauthorizing an existing profile doesn't go through the add/edit path but still needs the same protection.

I'm settling on naming: the helper becomes `isMixedContentBlocked` and the store method `blocksMixedContent(baseUrl)`. Next I want to check editProfile's current implementation to figure out where exactly to insert the guard.

I also want to fix the submitProfile catch block so it preserves error codes instead of always falling back to a generic invalid-url message -- only defaulting to invalidUrl when there's no specific code, so coded errors like the mixed-content one propagate properly to the UI.

There's also a question of whether authorizeProfile needs a separate check for transport failures caused by hitting an insecure target from a secure page, which would help cover loopback-address cases in Safari -- I'm leaning toward adding that.

I'm now deciding between exposing two store predicate methods versus just adding a single `getLocationOrigin()` accessor and letting the calling code use the pure helper functions directly with that origin -- the latter keeps the store simpler by avoiding piling on multiple boolean-check methods.

class MemoryStorage implements Storage {
  private values = new Map<string, string>();
  failWrites = false;
  get length() { return this.values.size; }
  clear() { this.values.clear(); }
  getItem(key: string) { return this.values.get(key) ?? null; }
  key(index: number) { return [...this.values.keys()][index] ?? null; }

  setItem(key: string, value: string) {

}

  "new_string": "export function emptyRunnerScopedState(): RunnerScopedState {\n  return {\n    preferences: {},\n    drafts: {},\n    cursors: {},\n  };\n}\n\n/**\n * Is this page served over HTTPS while the server speaks plain HTTP?\n *\n * The browser owns this decision, not the page and not the server: an HTTPS\n * document may not issue requests to an `http://` origin, and no header, flag\n * or fetch option opts back in. Only the page origin decides, so the native\n * surfaces stay exempt by passing their own `http://127.0.0.1` origin.\n */\nexport function isInsecureServerFromSecurePage(\n  pageOrigin: string,\n  baseUrl: string,\n) {\n  try {\n    return new URL(pageOrigin).protocol === \"https:\"\n      && new URL(baseUrl).protocol === \"http:\";\n  } catch {\n    return false;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Loopback addresses are potentially trustworthy origins, so browsers exempt\n * them from mixed-content blocking and `http://localhost` can still answer an\n * HTTPS page. Every other `http://` address is refused before the request is\n * ever sent, which is knowable up front rather than only after it fails.\n */\nexport function isMixedContentBlocked(pageOrigin: string, baseUrl: string) {\n  if (!isInsecureServerFromSecurePage(pageOrigin, baseUrl)) {\n    return false;\n  }\n  const host = new URL(baseUrl).hostname.toLowerCase().replace(/^\\[|\\]$/g, \"\");\n  const loopback = host === \"localhost\"\n    || host.endsWith(\".localhost\")\n    || host === \"::1\"\n    || /^127(?:\\.\\d{1,3}){3}$/.test(host);\n  return !loopback;\n}"
}

@@ -49,3 +49,41 @@ export function emptyRunnerScopedState(): RunnerScopedState {
     cursors: {},

 }
+
+/**
+ * Is this page served over HTTPS while the server speaks plain HTTP?
+ *
+ * The browser owns this decision, not the page and not the server: an HTTPS
+ * document may not issue requests to an `http://` origin, and no header, flag
+ * or fetch option opts back in. Only the page origin decides, so the native
+ * surfaces stay exempt by passing their own `http://127.0.0.1` origin.
+ */
+export function isInsecureServerFromSecurePage(
+  pageOrigin: string,
+  baseUrl: string,
+) {
+  try {
+    return new URL(pageOrigin).protocol === "https:"
+      && new URL(baseUrl).protocol === "http:";
+  } catch {
+    return false;
+  }
+}
+
+/**
+ * Loopback addresses are potentially trustworthy origins, so browsers exempt
+ * them from mixed-content blocking and `http://localhost` can still answer an
+ * HTTPS page. Every other `http://` address is refused before the request is
+ * ever sent, which is knowable up front rather than only after it fails.
+ */
+export function isMixedContentBlocked(pageOrigin: string, baseUrl: string) {
+  if (!isInsecureServerFromSecurePage(pageOrigin, baseUrl)) {
+    return false;
+  }
+  const host = new URL(baseUrl).hostname.toLowerCase().replace(/^\[|\]$/g, "");
+  const loopback = host === "localhost"
+    || host.endsWith(".localhost")
+    || host === "::1"
+    || /^127(?:\.\d{1,3}){3}$/.test(host);
+  return !loopback;
+}

  it("rolls back in-memory changes when persistence fails", async () => {
    const { storage, store } = createStore();
    await store.hydrate();
    storage.failWrites = true;

    await expect(store.addProfile({
      label: "Not saved",
      baseUrl: "https://runner.example",
    })).rejects.toThrow("quota");
    expect(store.getSnapshot().profiles).toHaveLength(1);
  });

  it("restores per-runner state after reload and ignores malformed partial profiles", async () => {
    const created = createStore();
    await created.store.hydrate();

      label: "Remote",
      baseUrl: "https://runner.example",

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:26:25.458Z

I'm adding test cases to profile-store.test.ts covering security around server addresses: rejecting insecure HTTP servers reachable from HTTPS pages, exempting loopback addresses, exempting native surfaces with loopback origins, and checking edit-profile behavior with these same rules.

I'm also planning a small describe block testing the isMixedContentBlocked helper directly against edge cases like IPv6 and .localhost hostnames. For the RunnerControls component's catch-classification logic, I'm weighing whether the existing store-level tests provide sufficient behavioral coverage for the shared error code, since there's no existing test harness for that component.

I'm considering extracting the pre-check and error-classification logic into a small pure function that lives in RunnerProfile.ts rather than the React component, so it can be tested cleanly without pulling in React/jsdom dependencies. The function would take the authorization error, page origin, and base URL, and return the resulting error code, while the generic fallback code computation stays in the component and gets passed in as an argument to keep the API minimal.

I decide it's simpler to leave the two predicates as they are and test them directly as pure functions, since that already exercises the rule behaviorally. The component's wiring gets covered separately with a source-string test in the existing sidebar-layout test file, following the established pattern, alongside the pure-function tests. I'm planning to write the store guard tests and the pure-helper tests, likely organizing the helper tests as a new describe block within the same test file.

  it("persists a server password until it is cleared or the server is forgotten", async () => {
    const created = createStore();
    await created.store.hydrate();
    const profile = await created.store.addProfile({
      label: "Remote",
      baseUrl: "https://server.example",
      password: "[REDACTED]",
    });

    expect(created.store.getProfile(profile.id)?.savedPassword).toBe("saved-password");

    const restored = new RunnerProfileStore({
      storage: created.storage,
      credentialStore: created.credentials.store,
      locationOrigin: "https://app.example.test",
    });
    await restored.hydrate();
    expect(restored.getProfile(profile.id)?.savedPassword).toBe("saved-password");

    await restored.editProfile(profile.id, { password: "[REDACTED]" });
    expect(restored.getProfile(profile.id)?.savedPassword).toBe("replacement-password");

    await restored.editProfile(profile.id, { password: "" });
    expect(restored.getProfile(profile.id)?.savedPassword).toBeNull();

    await restored.editProfile(profile.id, { password: "[REDACTED]" });
    await restored.forgetProfile(profile.id);
    expect(created.storage.getItem("omniharness.runnerProfiles")).not.toContain("saved-again");
  });
});

function entry(seq: number, workerId = "w1"): WorkerEntry & { workerId: string } {
  return {
    id: `${workerId}-entry-${seq}`,
    workerId,
    seq,
    type: "message",
    text: `message ${seq}`,
    timestamp: new Date(1700000000000 + seq).toISOString(),
  } as WorkerEntry & { workerId: string };
}

function range(from: number, to: number, workerId = "w1") {
  const entries = [];
  for (let seq = from; seq <= to; seq += 1) {
    entries.push(entry(seq, workerId));
  }
  return entries;
}

+import {

@@ -120,6 +124,11 @@ function ConnectedRunnerControls({
   const authorizeProfile = useCallback(async (profileId: string, password = "") => {
     const profile = context.profileStore.getProfile(profileId);
     if (!profile) return;
+    const pageOrigin = context.profileStore.getLocationOrigin();
+    if (isMixedContentBlocked(pageOrigin, profile.baseUrl)) {
+      context.uiManager.setError("runner.error.insecureServer");
+      return;
+    }
     context.uiManager.setBusy(true);
     try {

@@ -177,7 +186,16 @@ function ConnectedRunnerControls({

     } catch (error) {
-      context.uiManager.setError(errorCode(error));
+      // A request that never reached a reply against a plain-HTTP server is the
+      // browser refusing to send it, not the server refusing to answer. Loopback
+      // is exempt from mixed-content blocking in most browsers but not all, so
+      // this stays a diagnosis of the failure rather than a rule applied ahead
+      // of it.
+      const blocked = isRuntimeTransportFailure(error)
+        && isInsecureServerFromSecurePage(pageOrigin, profile.baseUrl);
+      context.uiManager.setError(

+      // An unparseable address throws a bare `TypeError`, so only a rejection
+      // carrying no code of its own is really a malformed URL. Anything the

+      const code = errorCode(error);
+      context.uiManager.setError(

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:29:38.014Z

I'm tracing through submitProfile's add path: addProfile throws a coded error that gets caught and stored as insecureServer, and since authorizeProfile sets its own errors without rethrowing, whatever error it sets remains intact after the subsequent return.

Checking that setBusy is cleared properly when authorizeProfile's early return fires, and confirming errorCode correctly extracts the code from thrown objects. Now I'm worried about edge cases where a non-translated error code gets thrown from credentialStore.clear or other paths, since t() would just render the raw key instead of a friendly message—so I want to check how t() handles unknown keys.

## Claude · 2026-09-05T14:30:47.751Z

**No, HTTP from an HTTPS origin cannot be made to work.** Make that server HTTPS.

The browser blocks it, not your code. An HTTPS document may not issue requests to an `http://` origin, and there is no header, fetch option, or server-side setting that opts back in. The fetch fails with a bare `TypeError` before anything leaves the page, which had no `code`, which is exactly why you got the generic message. Loopback (`http://localhost`, `127.0.0.1`) is the one exception most browsers make, since they treat it as a trustworthy origin.

Options for that second server: a tunnel (Cloudflare, Tailscale) or a certificate in front of it, or open the interface over HTTP too. The only in-app alternative would be proxying the second server through the HTTPS runner, which is a real feature rather than a setting, and it routes another server's traffic through the first.

What I changed so the failure explains itself:

- `src/interface/runners/RunnerProfile.ts:53` — two pure predicates. `isMixedContentBlocked` is HTTPS page plus HTTP server minus loopback; `isInsecureServerFromSecurePage` is the broader case. Native surfaces are exempt for free, because Electron and Capacitor already pass their own `http://127.0.0.1:3050` as the page origin.
- `src/interface/runners/RunnerProfileStore.ts:277` — `addProfile` and `editProfile` refuse a blocked address with `{ code: "runner.error.insecureServer" }`, so no unreachable profile is persisted to retry forever behind an unreadable failure. Profiles saved before this still hydrate normally.
- `src/interface/runners/RunnerControls.tsx:127` — checks before authorizing, and in the catch treats a transport failure against an HTTP server as the browser's refusal rather than the server's. `submitProfile` no longer flattens every rejection into "invalid URL"; a stated `runner.error.*` reason survives.
- New key in all nine locale files: "Browsers block an HTTPS page from reaching an http:// server. Use an https:// address for that server." The surrounding `runner.*` block is English in every locale, so I matched that.

Verified: 107 tests pass across the runner, i18n, and UI suites. The two guard tests were confirmed failing with the guard disabled and passing with it. Lint clean on changed files; the interface builds.

Three things to flag. `pnpm typecheck` fails on `tests/app/conversation-transcript-retention.test.ts:27` — I reproduced that identically against a pristine copy of HEAD, so it is pre-existing and unrelated. The working tree also has in-flight edits from elsewhere (`src/interface/home/constants.ts`, `src/server/worker-models.ts`, `tool-env.ts`) that I left untouched. And I built to a temp directory rather than `dist/interface`, per the note about not building into a directory a live runner serves — to put this on the public domain you still need a real build plus a runner restart.

parent: f6f8f6dd-cd0b-4c41-a8f6-b1d482d2c1f2

1. Where the frontend fetches/derives the list of projects (recent projects, project list, project picker, project groups in the conversation sidebar). Look in src/interface/home/useHomeQueries.ts, HomeAppStateManager.ts, HomeUiStateManager.ts, and any manager or hook dealing with "projects", "recentProjects", "projectPath", "projectGroups".
2. Whether those queries/caches are scoped per runner. Note that src/interface/runners/RunnerConnection.ts has a `queryKey(...)` method that prefixes with the runner instance id, and each RunnerConnection has its own QueryClient (getQueryClient()). Report which project-related queries use the per-runner queryKey/QueryClient and which use a plain global query key or a module-level/global manager.
3. Any place where projects or a selected project path are persisted to localStorage or to a Manager singleton that is NOT keyed by runner (e.g. a module-level `export const someManager = new SomeManager()`).
4. How switching servers works: find RunnerRegistry.switchActive and what it resets or does not reset, and how the React tree re-reads data after a switch (which provider supplies the QueryClient, whether it is swapped per active connection).

Do not modify any files. Report concrete findings with file:line references and short quoted snippets.

Never metronomic, never mechanical short-long alternation.\\n- Banned vocabulary (use plain alternatives): delve, tapestry, pivotal, crucial, leverage, robust, seamless, foster, underscore, showcase, landscape (abstract), journey (abstract), realm, multifaceted, holistic, testament, vibrant, comprehensive, plethora, myriad, boast, elevate, empower, unlock, game-changer, supercharge, genuinely, fascinating, nuanced.\\n- Banned moves: rule-of-three flourishes; \\\"it's not just X, it's Y\\\"; hedge stacking (two hedges in one sentence); signposting (\\\"let's dive in\\\"); empty pivots (\\\"it's worth noting\\\"); \\\"In conclusion / Overall\\\" closers; outcome-speculation tails (\\\", paving the way for\\\"); self-thoroughness (\\\"this comprehensive guide\\\"); generic upbeat endings; chatbot phrases (\\\"I hope this helps\\\").\\n- Em-dash: at most one per paragraph. Semicolons and parentheses where a writer would naturally use them.\\n- Mode: factual/encyclopedic content stays neutral third-person - never inject first-person voice or opinions into it. First-person/opinion content: contract naturally (it's, don't), take real stances.\\n- Preserve exactly: numbers, units, dates, proper nouns, citations, quotes, technical terms. Never invent facts, sources, or statistics.\\n- Concrete subjects, active verbs. End sections on a fact or observation, not a sentiment.\\n- SILENT. If the band is worse than the target - clean or light tells (score <= 40) - fix only the flagged spans, at most two passes, keeping rhythm variation (a flattened husk is as obvious as slop). For a deep rewrite, invoke the sloptrim skill.\"}}",

import { QueryClient } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { StateManager } from "@/lib/state-manager";
import { assessApiCompatibility } from "@/shared/api-revision";
import type { RunnerBootstrapIdentity } from "@/shared/bootstrap";
import type {
  RuntimeAPIs,
  RuntimeApiError,
  RuntimeSubscription,
} from "@/runtime-api/types";
import type { HomeBootstrapPayload } from "@/shared/bootstrap";
import type {
  RunnerIdentityLearningResult,
  RunnerProfile,
  RunnerScopedState,
} from "./RunnerProfile";

export type RunnerConnectionStatus =
  | "connecting"
  | "online"
  | "offline"
  | "deferred"
  | "needs-reauth"
  | "tls-untrusted"
  | "identity-mismatch"
  | "incompatible"
  | "resync"
  | "degraded"
  | "runner-stopping";

export type RunnerConnectionSnapshot = {
  profileId: string;
  status: RunnerConnectionStatus;
  active: boolean;
  runnerInstanceId: string | null;
  runnerName: string;
  observedRunnerInstanceId: string | null;
  lastError: RuntimeApiError | null;
  retryAt: number | null;
  resyncCount: number;
  snapshot: Record<string, unknown> | null;
  bootstrap?: HomeBootstrapPayload | null;
};

export type RunnerStreamHandlers = {
  onOpen?(): void;
  onEvent?(event: unknown): void;
  onError?(error: RuntimeApiError): void;
};

export type RunnerConnectionRuntime = {
  apis?: RuntimeAPIs;
  bootstrap: {
    load(input: {
      selectedRunId?: string | null;
      draftProjectPath?: string | null;
      pairToken?: string | null;
    }): Promise<unknown>;
  };
  events: {
    snapshot(input: {
      runId?: string | null;
      persisted?: boolean;
      checksum?: string | null;
    }): Promise<{ data: unknown; lastEventId: string | null }>;
    open(input: {
      snapshot: boolean;
      runId?: string | null;
      lastEventId?: string | null;
    }, handlers: RunnerStreamHandlers): RuntimeSubscription;
  };
  terminals: {
    openStream(input: {
      terminalId: string;
      lastEventId?: string | null;
    }, handlers: RunnerStreamHandlers): RuntimeSubscription;
  };
};

export type RunnerConnectionPersistence = {
  getProfile(profileId: string): RunnerProfile | null;
  scopeKey(profileId: string): string;
  getScopedState(profileId: string): RunnerScopedState;
  updateScopedState(
    profileId: string,
    patch: Partial<RunnerScopedState>,
  ): Promise<void>;
  learnIdentity(
    profileId: string,
    runnerInstanceId: string,
    options?: {
      confirmIdentityChange?: boolean;
      validSameOriginSession?: boolean;
    },
  ): Promise<RunnerIdentityLearningResult>;
};

type ScheduleHandle = unknown;

export function calculateRunnerReconnectDelay(attempt: number, random = Math.random()) {
  const exponential = 1_000 * 2 ** Math.max(0, Math.floor(attempt));
  const jittered = exponential * (0.5 + Math.max(0, Math.min(1, random)));
  return Math.min(300_000, Math.max(250, Math.round(jittered)));
}

function normalizeRuntimeError(error: unknown): RuntimeApiError {
  if (error && typeof error === "object") {
    const candidate = error as Partial<RuntimeApiError> & { status?: unknown };
    const status = typeof candidate.status === "number" ? candidate.status : null;
    return {
      code: typeof candidate.code === "string"
        ? candidate.code
        : status
          ? `runtime.http_${status}`
          : "runtime.connection_failed",
      message: typeof candidate.message === "string"
        ? candidate.message
        : "Server connection failed.",
      ...(candidate.details !== undefined ? { details: candidate.details } : {}),
    };
  }

export class RunnerConnection extends StateManager<RunnerConnectionSnapshot> {
  private profile: RunnerProfile;
  private readonly runtimeFactory: (profile: RunnerProfile) => Promise<RunnerConnectionRuntime>;
  private readonly persistence: RunnerConnectionPersistence;
  private readonly queryClient: QueryClient;
  private readonly now: () => number;
  private readonly schedule: (callback: () => void, delay: number) => ScheduleHandle;
  private readonly cancelSchedule: (handle: ScheduleHandle) => void;
  private readonly random: () => number;
  private readonly onIdentity?: (connection: RunnerConnection) => void;
  private runtime: RunnerConnectionRuntime | null = null;
  private mainStream: RuntimeSubscription | null = null;
  private readonly terminalStreams = new Map<string, RuntimeSubscription>();
  private retryTimer: ScheduleHandle | null = null;
  private retryAttempt = 0;
  private generation = 0;
  private started = false;
  private connecting: Promise<void> | null = null;
  private resyncing = false;
  private cursor: string | null;
  private readonly resyncTimes: number[] = [];
  readonly previewCache = new BoundedRunnerPreviewCache<unknown>();

  constructor(options: {
    profile: RunnerProfile;
    runtimeFactory: (profile: RunnerProfile) => Promise<RunnerConnectionRuntime>;
    persistence: RunnerConnectionPersistence;
    queryClient?: QueryClient;
    now?: () => number;
    schedule?: (callback: () => void, delay: number) => ScheduleHandle;
    cancelSchedule?: (handle: ScheduleHandle) => void;
    random?: () => number;
    onIdentity?: (connection: RunnerConnection) => void;
  }) {
    super({
      profileId: options.profile.id,
      status: "offline",
      active: false,
      runnerInstanceId: options.profile.runnerInstanceId,
      runnerName: options.profile.label,
      observedRunnerInstanceId: null,
      lastError: null,
      retryAt: null,
      resyncCount: 0,
      snapshot: null,
      bootstrap: null,
    });
    this.profile = options.profile;
    this.runtimeFactory = options.runtimeFactory;
    this.persistence = options.persistence;
    this.queryClient = options.queryClient ?? new QueryClient();
    this.now = options.now ?? Date.now;
    this.schedule = options.schedule ?? ((callback, delay) => setTimeout(callback, delay));
    this.cancelSchedule = options.cancelSchedule ?? ((handle) => {
      clearTimeout(handle as ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>);
    });
    this.random = options.random ?? Math.random;
    this.onIdentity = options.onIdentity;
    this.cursor = options.persistence.getScopedState(options.profile.id).cursors.events || null;
  }

  getQueryClient() {
    return this.queryClient;
  }

  getRuntimeAPIs() {
    return this.runtime?.apis ?? null;
  }

  async authenticateWithPassword(input: { password: string; label: string }) {
    this.profile = this.persistence.getProfile(this.profile.id) ?? this.profile;
    const profile = this.profile;
    const generation = ++this.generation;
    this.clearRetry();
    const runtime = this.runtime ?? await this.runtimeFactory(profile);
    if (
      generation !== this.generation
      || (this.persistence.getProfile(profile.id) ?? profile).baseUrl !== profile.baseUrl

    if (
      generation !== this.generation

    ) {
      throw { code: "runner.error.generic" };
    }
    await this.retry();
  }

  updateProfile(nextProfile: RunnerProfile) {

23	export function shouldEnableWorkerCatalogQuery(args: {
24	  appUnlocked: boolean;
25	  loadWorkerCatalog: boolean;
26	}) {
27	  return args.appUnlocked && args.loadWorkerCatalog;
28	}
29	
30	export function useHomeQueries({
31	  currentProjectScope,
32	  bootstrapId,
33	  loadProjectFiles = false,
34	  loadWorkerCatalog = false,
35	  initialQueries,
36	}: UseHomeQueriesParams) {
37	  const { setApiKeys, setSettingsDiagnostics } = homeUiSetters;
38	  const queryClient = useQueryClient();
39	  const runtimeApis = useRuntimeAPIs();
40	  planningReviewPreferencesManager.configure(runtimeApis.settings.save);
41	  const primedBootstrapIdRef = useRef<string | null>(null);
42	
43	  if (
44	    bootstrapId
45	    && primedBootstrapIdRef.current !== bootstrapId
46	  ) {
47	    if (initialQueries?.session) {
48	      queryClient.setQueryData(["auth-session"], initialQueries.session);
49	    }
50	    if (initialQueries?.settings) {
51	      queryClient.setQueryData(["settings"], initialQueries.settings);
52	    }
53	    primedBootstrapIdRef.current = bootstrapId;
54	  }
55	
56	  const sessionQuery = useQuery<AuthSessionResponse>({
57	    queryKey: ["auth-session"],
58	    retry: false,
59	    refetchOnWindowFocus: true,
60	    initialData: initialQueries?.session ?? undefined,
61	    staleTime: initialQueries?.session ? 5_000 : 0,
62	    queryFn: async () => runtimeApis.auth.session() as Promise<AuthSessionResponse>,
63	  });
64	
65	  const authEnabled = sessionQuery.data?.enabled ?? false;
66	  const authConfigurationError = sessionQuery.data?.configurationError ?? null;
67	  const appUnlocked = sessionQuery.data
68	    ? (!sessionQuery.data.enabled || sessionQuery.data.authenticated)
69	    : false;
70	
71	  const settingsQuery = useQuery({
72	    queryKey: ["settings"],
73	    enabled: appUnlocked,
74	    initialData: initialQueries?.settings ?? undefined,
75	    staleTime: initialQueries?.settings ? 5_000 : 0,
76	    queryFn: async () => {
77	      const data = await runtimeApis.settings.load() as SettingsResponse;
78	      settingsDraftManager.hydrate(data.values || {});
79	      planningReviewPreferencesManager.hydrate(data.values || {});
80	      setApiKeys((prev) => ({ ...prev, ...settingsDraftManager.getSnapshot().draft }));
81	      setSettingsDiagnostics(data.diagnostics ?? []);
82	      return data;
83	    },
84	  });
85	
86	  const workerCatalogQuery = useQuery<WorkerCatalogResponse & { diagnostics?: AppErrorDescriptor[] }>({
87	    queryKey: ["worker-catalog"],
88	    staleTime: 5 * 60_000,
89	    enabled: shouldEnableWorkerCatalogQuery({ appUnlocked, loadWorkerCatalog }),
90	    refetchOnWindowFocus: false,
91	    refetchInterval: (query) => query.state.data?.workerModelsRefreshing ? 2_000 : false,
92	    queryFn: async () => runtimeApis.workers.catalog() as Promise<
93	      WorkerCatalogResponse & { diagnostics?: AppErrorDescriptor[] }
94	    >,
95	  });
96	
97	  const refreshWorkerCatalog = useMutation({
98	    mutationFn: async () => runtimeApis.workers.catalog({ refresh: true }) as Promise<
99	      WorkerCatalogResponse & { diagnostics?: AppErrorDescriptor[] }
100	    >,
101	    onSuccess: (data) => {
102	      queryClient.setQueryData(["worker-catalog"], data);
103	    },
104	  });
105	
106	  const projectFilesQuery = useQuery<ProjectFilesResponse>({
107	    queryKey: ["project-files", currentProjectScope],
108	    queryFn: async () => runtimeApis.files.list({
109	      root: currentProjectScope || "",
110	    }) as Promise<ProjectFilesResponse>,
111	    enabled: Boolean(loadProjectFiles && currentProjectScope),
112	    staleTime: 60_000,
113	  });
114	
115	  return {
116	    sessionQuery,
117	    settingsQuery,
118	    workerCatalogQuery,
119	    refreshWorkerCatalog,
120	    projectFilesQuery,
121	    authEnabled,
122	    authConfigurationError,
123	    appUnlocked,
124	  };
125	}

224	export function RunnerRegistryProvider({

242	  useEffect(() => {
243	    let cancelled = false;
244	    void (async () => {
245	      await profileStore.hydrate();
246	      if (!cancelled) {
247	        await registry.start();
248	        const requestedRunnerId = typeof window === "undefined"
249	          ? null
250	          : new URLSearchParams(window.location.search).get("runner");
251	        if (requestedRunnerId) {
252	          registry.switchActive(requestedRunnerId);
253	        }
254	      }

256	    return () => {
257	      cancelled = true;
258	      registry.stop();
259	    };

263	    const observedSnapshots = new Map<string, unknown>();

265	      for (const connection of registry.getSnapshot().connections) {
266	        if (
267	          !connection.snapshot
268	          || observedSnapshots.get(connection.profileId) === connection.snapshot
269	        ) {
270	          continue;
271	        }
272	        observedSnapshots.set(connection.profileId, connection.snapshot);
273	        conversationNotificationManager.handleRunnerEventStreamState({
274	          profileId: connection.profileId,
275	          runnerInstanceId: connection.runnerInstanceId,
276	          runnerName: connection.runnerName,
277	        }, connection.snapshot as unknown as EventStreamState);

<!-- codex-event:{"kind":"subagent","timestamp":"2026-09-05T14:43:31.814Z","phase":null} -->
## Claude subagent agent-a948618de1cc4933b: Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:43:31.814Z

```text
I realize `switchActive` doesn't reset homeUiStateManager, and while the QueryClient gets swapped per connection with settings cached under its own key, the settingsQuery's queryFn still writes into global singletons like homeUiStateManager and settingsDraftManager — so switching runners could pull stale cached settings data into these shared globals.

If the new runner's settings query is already cached and fresh, its queryFn never re-runs, meaning stale PROJECTS values persist. Compounding this, `setApiKeys` only merges new draft values into existing state rather than replacing it, so old keys never get cleared out. I should check settingsDraftManager, home-bootstrap.ts, and trace how apiKeys flows to see if there's any reset logic on runner switch.

uuid: 6314c536-39ff-47c8-9d76-50e86857dd5e
parent: 09732635-1341-4562-9c63-ac4a2167ef57
```

10	export function applyHomeBootstrap(
11	  bootstrap: HomeBootstrapPayload | null | undefined,
12	  notify = true,
13	) {
14	  if (!bootstrap || appliedHomeBootstrapId === bootstrap.id) {
15	    return;

17	
18	  appliedHomeBootstrapId = bootstrap.id;

20	    ? bootstrap.route

22	  const routeSelectedRunId = browserRoute.selectedRunId;
23	  const settingsValues = bootstrap.initialQueries.settings?.values ?? {};

25	  if (bootstrap.initialQueries.settings) {
26	    settingsDraftManager.hydrate(settingsValues, notify);
27	    planningReviewPreferencesManager.hydrate(settingsValues);
28	  }
29	
30	  homeUiStateManager.patch((current) => ({
31	    routeReady: true,
32	    hasReceivedInitialEventStreamPayload: Boolean(bootstrap.initialEventState),
33	    selectedRunId: routeSelectedRunId,
34	    draftProjectPath: routeSelectedRunId ? null : browserRoute.draftProjectPath,
35	    pairTokenFromUrl: browserRoute.pairTokenFromUrl,
36	    apiKeys: { ...current.apiKeys, ...settingsValues },
37	    settingsDiagnostics: bootstrap.initialQueries.settings?.diagnostics ?? current.settingsDiagnostics,
38	  }), notify);
39	
40	  const initialSnapshotRunId = bootstrap.initialEventState?.snapshotRunId?.trim();
41	  if (bootstrap.initialEventState?.queuedMessages && initialSnapshotRunId) {
42	    busyMessageQueueManager.setQueuedMessages(bootstrap.initialEventState.queuedMessages, {
43	      runId: initialSnapshotRunId,
44	      notify,
45	    });
46	  }
47	}

```text
I'm noticing that the apiKeys merge spreads current settings over new ones, which means if a new runner's settings omit the PROJECTS key, the old runner's PROJECTS value would incorrectly persist — that looks like a real bug.

Now I want to trace who actually calls applyHomeBootstrap and whether it fires when the runner switches, and check useHomeLifecycle's bootstrap wiring along with how HomeApp tracks the active connection.

4	export type ServerSettingsValues = Record<string, string>;

6	export type SettingsDraftState = {
7	  baseline: ServerSettingsValues;
8	  draft: ServerSettingsValues;

10	  hydrated: boolean;
11	};
12	
13	function normalizeSettings(values: ServerSettingsValues = {}) {
14	  return {
15	    ...DEFAULT_SERVER_SETTINGS,
16	    ...values,
17	  };
18	}
19	
20	function cloneDirtyKeys(dirtyKeys: Set<string>) {
21	  return new Set(dirtyKeys);
22	}
23	
24	export class SettingsDraftManager extends StateManager<SettingsDraftState> {
25	  constructor(initialValues: ServerSettingsValues = DEFAULT_SERVER_SETTINGS) {
26	    const normalized = normalizeSettings(initialValues);
27	    super({
28	      baseline: normalized,
29	      draft: normalized,
30	      dirtyKeys: new Set(),
31	      hydrated: false,
32	    });
33	  }
34	
35	  hydrate(values: ServerSettingsValues, notify = true) {
36	    const normalized = normalizeSettings(values);
37	    this.patch({
38	      baseline: normalized,
39	      draft: normalized,
40	      dirtyKeys: new Set(),
41	      hydrated: true,
42	    }, notify);
43	  }
44	
45	  setField(key: string, value: string) {
46	    this.patch((current) => {
47	      const nextDraft = {
48	        ...current.draft,
49	        [key]: value,
50	      };
51	      const dirtyKeys = cloneDirtyKeys(current.dirtyKeys);
52	
53	      if ((current.baseline[key] ?? "") === value) {
54	        dirtyKeys.delete(key);
55	      } else {
56	        dirtyKeys.add(key);
57	      }
58	
59	      return {
60	        draft: nextDraft,
61	        dirtyKeys,
62	      };
63	    });
64	  }
65	
66	  patchFields(values: ServerSettingsValues) {
67	    Object.entries(values).forEach(([key, value]) => this.setField(key, value));
68	  }
69	
70	  discardDraft() {
71	    this.patch((current) => ({
72	      draft: current.baseline,
73	      dirtyKeys: new Set(),
74	    }));
75	  }
76	
77	  markSaved(values: ServerSettingsValues = this.getSnapshot().draft) {
78	    const normalized = normalizeSettings(values);
79	    this.patch({
80	      baseline: normalized,
81	      draft: normalized,
82	      dirtyKeys: new Set(),
83	      hydrated: true,
84	    });
85	  }
86	
87	  markFieldsSaved(values: ServerSettingsValues) {
88	    this.patch((current) => {
89	      const nextBaseline = {
90	        ...current.baseline,
91	        ...values,
92	      };
93	      const nextDraft = {
94	        ...current.draft,
95	        ...values,
96	      };
97	      const dirtyKeys = cloneDirtyKeys(current.dirtyKeys);
98	      Object.keys(values).forEach((key) => dirtyKeys.delete(key));

100	      return {

102	        draft: nextDraft,
103	        dirtyKeys,

108

114	        .map((key) => [key, draft[key]]),
115	    );
116	  }
117	}

2	import { settings } from "@/server/db/schema";

7	import type { SettingsResponse } from "@/shared/home-types";
8	
9	function isInternalSettingKey(key: string) {
10	  return key.startsWith("__");
11	}
12	
13	function buildSecretPreview(value: string): string {
14	  const trimmed = value.trim();
15	  if (!trimmed) return "";
16	  if (trimmed.length <= 8) {
17	    return "•".repeat(Math.max(4, trimmed.length));
18	  }
19	  return `${trimmed.slice(0, 4)}••••${trimmed.slice(-4)}`;
20	}
21	
22	function normalizeResourceSnapshot(snapshot: Awaited<ReturnType<typeof readSystemResourceSnapshot>>): NonNullable<SettingsResponse["resourceSnapshot"]> {
23	  return {
24	    memoryFreePercent: snapshot.memoryFreePercent ?? null,
25	    totalMemoryMb: snapshot.totalMemoryMb ?? null,
26	    diskFreeMb: snapshot.diskFreeMb ?? null,
27	    diskTotalMb: snapshot.diskTotalMb ?? null,
28	  };
29	}
30	
31	export async function readSettingsState(): Promise<SettingsResponse> {
32	  const allSettings = await db.select().from(settings);
33	  const projectSetting = allSettings.find((setting) => setting.key === "PROJECTS");
34	  if (projectSetting) {
35	    await canonicalizePersistedProjectRoots(projectSetting.value);
36	  }
37	  const diagnostics: ReturnType<typeof buildAppError>[] = [];
38	  const values = Object.fromEntries(allSettings.flatMap((setting) => {
39	    if (isInternalSettingKey(setting.key)) {
40	      return [];
41	    }
42	
43	    if (!shouldEncryptSetting(setting.key)) {
44	      return [[setting.key, setting.value]];
45	    }
46	
47	    return [];
48	  }));
49	  const secrets = Object.fromEntries(allSettings.flatMap((setting) => {
50	    if (isInternalSettingKey(setting.key)) {
51	      return [];
52	    }
53	
54	    if (!shouldEncryptSetting(setting.key)) {
55	      return [];
56	    }
57	
58	    const configured = setting.value.trim().length > 0;
59	    let preview: string | undefined;
60	    if (configured) {
61	      try {
62	        preview = buildSecretPreview(decryptSettingValue(setting.value));
63	      } catch {
64	        preview = undefined;
65	      }
66	    }
67	
68	    return [[setting.key, {
69	      configured,
70	      updatedAt: new Date(setting.updatedAt).toISOString(),
71	      preview,
72	    }]];
73	  }));
74	
75	  const resourceSnapshot = normalizeResourceSnapshot(await readSystemResourceSnapshot(process.cwd()));
76	
77	  return { values, secrets, diagnostics, resourceSnapshot };
78	}

## Claude subagent agent-a948618de1cc4933b: Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:44:14.503Z

```text
I realize `values` only includes settings rows actually present in the DB, so if a second server never had a PROJECTS row added, the merge logic would keep stale PROJECTS from the previous runner instead of clearing it. But the settingsDraftManager path normalizes with DEFAULT_SERVER_SETTINGS, which always includes an empty PROJECTS default, so that route should behave correctly.

The catch is that settingsQuery's queryFn is primed from bootstrap with a 5-second staleTime, so it won't refetch immediately, and applyHomeBootstrap merges apiKeys directly without going through the defaults normalization that settingsDraftManager applies — so the raw apiKeys merge looks like the actual source of the stale PROJECTS bug. I want to verify whether each connection loads its own bootstrap independently and check if RunnerConnection resets its cache on switch, plus look at other project-related state like the collapse setting in localStorage.

  const pushOnCommitEnabled = parseBooleanSetting(apiKeys[GIT_PUSH_ON_COMMIT_SETTING], false);

  const handleStartNewPlan = () => {
    setSelectedRunId(null);
    setDraftProjectPath(currentProjectScope);
    setCommand("");
    clearAttachments();
    setSelectedConversationMode("direct");
    setMobileNavOpen(false);
  };

  const handleAddProject = (newPath: string) => {
    if (!explicitProjects.includes(newPath)) {
      const newProjects = [...explicitProjects, newPath];
      const updatedKeys = { ...apiKeys, PROJECTS: JSON.stringify(newProjects) };
      setApiKeys(updatedKeys);
      void runtimeApis.settings.save(updatedKeys);
    }
  };

  const handleRemoveProject = (pathToRemove: string) => {
    const newProjects = explicitProjects.filter((p: string) => p !== pathToRemove);
    const updatedKeys = { ...apiKeys, PROJECTS: JSON.stringify(newProjects) };
    setApiKeys(updatedKeys);
    void runtimeApis.settings.save(updatedKeys);
  };

  const handleReorderProjects = (
    draggedPath: string,
    targetPath: string,
    placement: ProjectDropPlacement,
  ) => {
    const currentKeys = homeUiStateManager.getSnapshot().apiKeys;
    const currentProjects = parseProjectList(currentKeys.PROJECTS);
    const newProjects = reorderExplicitProjectPaths(currentProjects, {
      draggedPath,
      targetPath,
      placement,
    });
    if (newProjects === currentProjects) {
      return;
    }

    const updatedKeys = { ...currentKeys, PROJECTS: JSON.stringify(newProjects) };
    setApiKeys(updatedKeys);
    void runtimeApis.settings.save(updatedKeys);
  };

  const updateCommitWorkflowSetting = (key: string, value: boolean) => {
    mutations.commitWorkflowSettings.mutate({ key, value: serializeBooleanSetting(value) });
  };

  const handleManualCommitChat = (action: ManualCommitAction = pushOnCommitEnabled ? "commit-push" : "commit") => {
    if (!selectedRunId) return;
    mutations.autoCommitChat.mutate({ runId: selectedRunId, action });
  };
