16	
17	export function parseInterfaceRoute(location: Pick<Location, "pathname" | "search">) {
18	  const params = new URLSearchParams(location.search);
19	  const queryRunId = params.get("run")?.trim() || null;
20	  const sessionMatch = location.pathname.match(/^\/session\/([^/]+)\/?$/);
21	  const pathRunId = sessionMatch
22	    ? decodeURIComponent(sessionMatch[1] ?? "").trim() || null
23	    : null;
24	  return {
25	    selectedRunId: queryRunId ?? pathRunId,
26	    draftProjectPath: params.get("project"),
27	    pairToken: params.get("pair"),
28	  };
29	}
30	
31	export function parseInlineBootstrap(text: string | null) {
32	  if (!text?.trim()) {
33	    return null;
34	  }
35	  return JSON.parse(text) as HomeBootstrapPayload;
36	}
37	
38	function createShellBootstrap(
39	  runtimeApis: ReturnType<typeof createRendererRuntimeAPIs>,
40	  route: ReturnType<typeof parseInterfaceRoute>,
41	): HomeBootstrapPayload {
42	  return {
43	    id: `${runtimeApis.runtime.surface}-shell`,
44	    route: {
45	      selectedRunId: route.selectedRunId,
46	      draftProjectPath: route.draftProjectPath,
47	      pairTokenFromUrl: route.pairToken,
48	    },
49	    initialEventState: null,
50	    initialLastEventId: "",
51	    initialQueries: { session: null, settings: null },
52	    features: { unifiedWorkerStream: true },
53	    runner: {
54	      runnerInstanceId: `${runtimeApis.runtime.surface}-shell`,
55	      name: runtimeApis.runtime.label,
56	      version: "0.1.0",
57	      apiRevision: { minimum: 1, current: 1 },
58	      capabilities: [],
59	      bridgeState: "degraded",
60	      readinessState: "starting",
61	      streamEpoch: runtimeApis.runtime.surface,
62	    },
63	  };
64	}
65	
66	async function mount() {
67	  const root = document.getElementById("root");
68	  if (!root) {
69	    return;
70	  }
71	
72	  root.textContent = t("boot.loading.message");
73	  try {
74	    const runtimeApis = createRendererRuntimeAPIs();
75	    if (window.location.pathname === "/authorize-interface") {
76	      const params = new URLSearchParams(window.location.search);
77	      createRoot(root).render(
78	        <AppErrorBoundary>
79	          <Providers>
80	            <RuntimeApiProvider apis={runtimeApis}>
81	              <InterfaceAuthorizationScreen request={{
82	                origin: params.get("origin") ?? "",
83	                state: params.get("state") ?? "",
84	                challenge: params.get("challenge") ?? "",
85	                method: params.get("method") ?? "",
86	              }} />
87	            </RuntimeApiProvider>
88	          </Providers>
89	        </AppErrorBoundary>,
90	      );
91	      return;
92	    }
93	    const route = parseInterfaceRoute(window.location);
94	    let bootstrap = (
95	      runtimeApis.runtime.surface === "electron"
96	      || runtimeApis.runtime.surface === "capacitor"
97	    )
98	      ? createShellBootstrap(runtimeApis, route)
99	      : parseInlineBootstrap(
100	        document.getElementById("omni-bootstrap")?.textContent ?? null,
101	      );
102	    if (!bootstrap) {
103	      try {
104	        bootstrap = await runtimeApis.bootstrap.load(route) as HomeBootstrapPayload;
105	      } catch {
106	        bootstrap = createShellBootstrap(runtimeApis, route);
107	      }
108	    }
109	    createRoot(root).render(
110	      <>
111	        <BugDropBootstrap />
112	        <PwaBootstrap />
113	        <AppErrorBoundary>
114	          <Providers>
115	            <TooltipProvider>
116	              <OmniApp bootstrap={bootstrap} runtimeApis={runtimeApis} />
117	            </TooltipProvider>
118	          </Providers>
119	        </AppErrorBoundary>
120	      </>,
121	    );
122	  } catch (error) {
123	    root.textContent = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);
124	  }
125	}
126	
127	if (typeof document !== "undefined") {
128	  void mount();
129	}

## Claude subagent agent-a948618de1cc4933b: Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:45:24.898Z

```text
I'm confirming that settings priming uses the query client tied to the new connection after switching, and that the HomeApp remount via key ensures a fresh ref. I'm also noting that with initialData and a 5s staleTime, the settings query will be treated as fresh and skip an immediate refetch.

I suspect this is why PROJECTS could persist incorrectly — the bootstrap merge only updates apiKeys with settingsValues, so if server B never had a PROJECTS entry written, server A's stale value would carry over. I want to check whether bootstrap ids are stable per runner or shared across connections, since a constant shell id could prevent proper reapplication when switching between servers.

```

export async function buildRuntimeBootstrap({
  searchParams = {},
  requestHeaders,
  includeInitialData = true,
}: RuntimeBootstrapOptions): Promise<HomeBootstrapPayload> {
  const requestUrl = buildRequestUrl(searchParams, requestHeaders);
  const selectedRunId = normalizeParam(searchParams.run);
  const draftProjectPath = normalizeParam(searchParams.project);
  const pairTokenFromUrl = normalizeParam(searchParams.pair);

  const session = await buildAuthSessionState({
    url: requestUrl.toString(),
    headers: requestHeaders,
  });
  const appUnlocked = Boolean(session && (!session.enabled || session.authenticated));

  const [runner, [settings, initialEventState]] = await Promise.all([
    buildRunnerBootstrapIdentity(),
    appUnlocked && includeInitialData
      ? Promise.all([
        readSettingsState(),
        buildPersistedEventPayload({ selectedRunId }),
      ])
      : Promise.resolve([null, null] as const),
  ]);

  return {
    id: JSON.stringify({
      builtAt: Date.now(),
      selectedRunId,
      draftProjectPath,
      pairTokenFromUrl,
      sessionAuthenticated: session?.authenticated ?? false,
      eventRunCount: initialEventState?.runs?.length ?? 0,
    }),
    route: {

## Claude subagent agent-a948618de1cc4933b: Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:45:33.894Z

```text
Since IDs are always unique with the timestamp, applyHomeBootstrap always applies — but I'm noticing a bug path: if the second server is locked and settings comes back null, the hydrate step gets skipped and merging with an empty object means server A's PROJECTS apiKeys stay in place unexpectedly. I want to double check where applyHomeBootstrap gets called during HomeApp's render to confirm this.

That call passes notify=false, so subscribers aren't notified, but since it's during a remount, useSyncExternalStore still reads a fresh snapshot so that's fine. The runner's event state is seeded per HomeApp instance from bootstrap.initialEventState, which also checks out. I think I have enough context now — including confirming that ExternalSessionsPicker's query key is a global but per-runner-client list — to write up the final report.



uuid: a29ff56d-5dc9-4369-90c9-de25b5fddfd4
parent: 09fe26fc-dc71-4f9f-9946-3664bac9d744
```

## Claude subagent agent-a948618de1cc4933b: Claude · 2026-09-05T14:46:12.053Z

````text
I have enough to report. Here are the findings.

## 1. Where the project list comes from

The sidebar's project groups are derived from a single settings value, `PROJECTS`, held in a **global** UI state manager — not from any per-runner query.

- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/useHomeViewModel.ts:102` — `const explicitProjects = useMemo(() => parseProjectList(apiKeys.PROJECTS), [apiKeys.PROJECTS]);`
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/useHomeViewModel.ts:202-206` — `buildConversationGroups({ explicitProjects, plans, runs })` → `groupedProjects`, then `filteredProjects` (208) and `activeProjects` (222), returned at 707-709.
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/HomeApp.tsx:490` — the same `parseProjectList(apiKeys.PROJECTS)` again, used to compute `currentProjectScope` (491-497) that feeds queries.
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/lib/conversations.ts:72-112` — groups are seeded from `normalizeExplicitProjectPaths(args.explicitProjects)`; runs only add extra groups.
- `apiKeys` itself: `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/HomeUiStateManager.ts:55` (`apiKeys: Record<string, string>`), default at 114, setter at 377.
- Write path: `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/useConversationActions.ts:96-131` (`handleAddProject`, `handleRemoveProject`, `handleReorderProjects`).

`useHomeQueries.ts` has no projects query at all. Its only project-shaped query is the file tree:
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/useHomeQueries.ts:106-113` — `queryKey: ["project-files", currentProjectScope]`.

`HomeAppStateManager.ts` contains no project state (only `autoResumeExhaustionManager`, line 26).

## 2. Per-runner scoping of the queries

`RunnerConnection.queryKey(...)` at `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/runners/RunnerConnection.ts:310-312` is **dead code** — `grep -rn "\.queryKey(" src` returns zero call sites. Every query in the app uses a plain global key:

| key | file:line |
|---|---|
| `["settings"]` (source of `PROJECTS`) | `useHomeQueries.ts:72`, primed at `:51` |
| `["auth-session"]` | `useHomeQueries.ts:57` |
| `["worker-catalog"]` | `useHomeQueries.ts:87` |
| `["project-files", scope]` | `useHomeQueries.ts:107`, `ComposerContainer.tsx:146` |
| `["external-sessions"]` | `ExternalSessionsPicker.tsx:68` |
| `["fs", currentPath]` | `FolderPickerDialog.tsx:40` |
| `["project-file", root, relativePath]` | `FileViewerPanel.tsx:44` |
| `["attachable-files", rootPath]` | `FileAttachmentPickerDialog.tsx:48` |

Cache isolation therefore rests entirely on the swapped QueryClient (see §4), and any state that leaves the query cache for a module-level manager loses its runner scoping.

## 3. Unscoped persistence / singletons (the likely root cause)

**`homeUiStateManager` is a module-level singleton that survives runner switches, and its `apiKeys` are merged, never replaced.**

- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/HomeUiStateManager.ts:366` — `export const homeUiStateManager = new HomeUiStateManager();`
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/home-bootstrap.ts:36` — `apiKeys: { ...current.apiKeys, ...settingsValues },`

That merge is the failure point. `readSettingsState` only returns keys that exist as rows in that runner's DB:
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/settings/read.ts:38-48` — `values` is built from `allSettings` rows only.

So if server B has never written a `PROJECTS` row (or if B is auth-locked, in which case `settings` is `null` — `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/runtime/bootstrap.ts:61-68` returns `[null, null]` when `!appUnlocked`, and `home-bootstrap.ts:25` then skips hydration entirely), `settingsValues.PROJECTS` is absent and **server A's `PROJECTS` string stays in `apiKeys`**, so A's project groups keep rendering under B.

The recovery path via the settings query is deferred, not immediate:
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/useHomeQueries.ts:74-80` — `initialData: initialQueries?.settings ?? undefined, staleTime: initialQueries?.settings ? 5_000 : 0`. With `initialData` present the query starts fresh, so the `queryFn` (the only place that does the defaults-normalized `setApiKeys((prev) => ({ ...prev, ...settingsDraftManager.getSnapshot().draft }))`, line 80) does **not** run on mount. Note it is also a merge, though `settingsDraftManager.hydrate` normalizes against `DEFAULT_SERVER_SETTINGS` (`SettingsDraftManager.ts:13-18, 35-43`), which includes `PROJECTS: "[]"` (`constants.ts:143`) — that path would have healed it, if it ran.

Related consequence in the same area: `handleAddProject` / `handleRemoveProject` post the entire merged `apiKeys` blob to the new server (`useConversationActions.ts:99-101`, `:107-109` — `void runtimeApis.settings.save(updatedKeys)`), so adding a project on server B can write server A's whole settings set into B.

Other module-level singletons holding per-server data, none keyed by runner:
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/SettingsDraftManager.ts:119`
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/GitWorkspaceManager.ts:463`, cache key `"omni-git-workspace-cache:v1"` (`:109`), indexed by `projectPath` only (`:225`, `:454`) — identical paths on two servers collide
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/ProjectMemoryPanelManager.ts:271`
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/WorkerEntriesManager.ts:881`, `PlanningReviewPreferencesManager.ts:89`, `BusyMessageQueueManager.ts:419`, `SessionStateManager.ts:50`, `GoalPlanManager.ts:265`, `ConversationTranscriptManager.ts:384`

localStorage, unscoped by runner:
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/useHomeLifecycle.ts:340` and `:457` — `"omni-collapsed-projects"` stores collapsed project paths globally
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/constants.ts:199-205` — composer worker/model/mode keys
- Contrast with what *is* scoped: `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/runners/RunnerProfileStore.ts:290-315` — `scopeKey()` returns `runnerInstanceId ?? id` and `scopedState` is stored per key. `PROJECTS` never goes through this.

## 4. How switching servers works

`/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/runners/RunnerRegistry.ts:130-143`:

```ts
switchActive(profileId: string, persist = true) {
  if (!this.connections.has(profileId)) return false;
  for (const [id, connection] of this.connections) connection.setActive(id === profileId);
  this.patch({ activeRunnerId: profileId }, false);
  this.publish();
  if (persist) void this.profileStore.setActiveRunner?.(profileId);
  return true;
}
```

It flips `active` flags and publishes. It does **not** reset any home/UI manager, does not clear `apiKeys`, and does not invalidate any query cache. `RunnerConnection.setActive` (`RunnerConnection.ts:654-659`) only closes terminal streams when going inactive.

React re-read path:
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/runners/RunnerRegistryProvider.tsx:236-240` — the provider subscribes via `useRegistrySnapshot`, and `const queryClient = activeConnection?.getQueryClient() ?? fallbackQueryClient;`
- `:298-300` — `<QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>`. So the QueryClient **is** swapped per active connection (each connection creates its own at `RunnerConnection.ts:250`).
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/ui/OmniApp.tsx:73-77` — `<HomeApp key={connectionSnapshot.profileId} bootstrap={bootstrap} runnerConnection={connection} />`, so HomeApp remounts on switch. React state resets; module-level managers do not.
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/HomeApp.tsx:210` — `applyHomeBootstrap(bootstrap, false)` runs during that render, and its `appliedHomeBootstrapId` guard (`home-bootstrap.ts:8, 14`) does pass, since bootstrap ids embed `Date.now()` (`src/runtime/bootstrap.ts:71-79`). The guard is not the problem; the `{ ...current.apiKeys, ...settingsValues }` merge at `home-bootstrap.ts:36` is.
- Also note an outer, module-level QueryClient at `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/providers.tsx:14, 27` wrapping the whole tree (`src/ui/render-web.tsx:114`); the per-runner one nests inside it, so anything rendered outside `RunnerRegistryProvider` shares one global cache.

**Shortest fix point:** replace the merge at `home-bootstrap.ts:36` with a runner-scoped reset (e.g. normalize `settingsValues` against `DEFAULT_SERVER_SETTINGS` the way `SettingsDraftManager.hydrate` does, and clear `apiKeys` when `bootstrap.initialQueries.settings` is null), and/or scope `homeUiStateManager`'s settings-derived slice by `profileId`.

uuid: 6bd77855-bc70-4c56-8345-a105a92fb285
parent: a29ff56d-5dc9-4369-90c9-de25b5fddfd4
````

10	export function applyHomeBootstrap(
11	  bootstrap: HomeBootstrapPayload | null | undefined,
12	  notify = true,
13	) {
14	  if (!bootstrap || appliedHomeBootstrapId === bootstrap.id) {
15	    return;
16	  }
17	
18	  appliedHomeBootstrapId = bootstrap.id;
19	  const browserRoute = typeof window === "undefined"

22	  const routeSelectedRunId = browserRoute.selectedRunId;
23	  const settingsValues = bootstrap.initialQueries.settings?.values ?? {};

26	    settingsDraftManager.hydrate(settingsValues, notify);
27	    planningReviewPreferencesManager.hydrate(settingsValues);
28	  }
29	
30	  homeUiStateManager.patch((current) => ({
31	    routeReady: true,

33	    selectedRunId: routeSelectedRunId,
34	    draftProjectPath: routeSelectedRunId ? null : browserRoute.draftProjectPath,
35	    pairTokenFromUrl: browserRoute.pairTokenFromUrl,
36	    apiKeys: { ...current.apiKeys, ...settingsValues },
37	    settingsDiagnostics: bootstrap.initialQueries.settings?.diagnostics ?? current.settingsDiagnostics,
38	  }), notify);
39	
40	  const initialSnapshotRunId = bootstrap.initialEventState?.snapshotRunId?.trim();
41	  if (bootstrap.initialEventState?.queuedMessages && initialSnapshotRunId) {
42	    busyMessageQueueManager.setQueuedMessages(bootstrap.initialEventState.queuedMessages, {
43	      runId: initialSnapshotRunId,
44	      notify,
45	    });
46	  }
47	}

9	function isInternalSettingKey(key: string) {
10	  return key.startsWith("__");
11	}
12	
13	function buildSecretPreview(value: string): string {
14	  const trimmed = value.trim();
15	  if (!trimmed) return "";
16	  if (trimmed.length <= 8) {
17	    return "•".repeat(Math.max(4, trimmed.length));
18	  }
19	  return `${trimmed.slice(0, 4)}••••${trimmed.slice(-4)}`;
20	}
21	
22	function normalizeResourceSnapshot(snapshot: Awaited<ReturnType<typeof readSystemResourceSnapshot>>): NonNullable<SettingsResponse["resourceSnapshot"]> {
23	  return {
24	    memoryFreePercent: snapshot.memoryFreePercent ?? null,
25	    totalMemoryMb: snapshot.totalMemoryMb ?? null,
26	    diskFreeMb: snapshot.diskFreeMb ?? null,
27	    diskTotalMb: snapshot.diskTotalMb ?? null,
28	  };
29	}

31	export async function readSettingsState(): Promise<SettingsResponse> {
32	  const allSettings = await db.select().from(settings);
33	  const projectSetting = allSettings.find((setting) => setting.key === "PROJECTS");
34	  if (projectSetting) {
35	    await canonicalizePersistedProjectRoots(projectSetting.value);
36	  }
37	  const diagnostics: ReturnType<typeof buildAppError>[] = [];
38	  const values = Object.fromEntries(allSettings.flatMap((setting) => {
39	    if (isInternalSettingKey(setting.key)) {
40	      return [];
41	    }
42	
43	    if (!shouldEncryptSetting(setting.key)) {
44	      return [[setting.key, setting.value]];
45	    }
46	
47	    return [];
48	  }));
49	  const secrets = Object.fromEntries(allSettings.flatMap((setting) => {
50	    if (isInternalSettingKey(setting.key)) {
51	      return [];
52	    }
53	
54	    if (!shouldEncryptSetting(setting.key)) {
55	      return [];
56	    }
57	
58	    const configured = setting.value.trim().length > 0;
59	    let preview: string | undefined;
60	    if (configured) {
61	      try {
62	        preview = buildSecretPreview(decryptSettingValue(setting.value));
63	      } catch {
64	        preview = undefined;
65	      }
66	    }
67	
68	    return [[setting.key, {
69	      configured,
70	      updatedAt: new Date(setting.updatedAt).toISOString(),
71	      preview,
72	    }]];
73	  }));
74

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:46:59.072Z

I found the inconsistency: `homeUiStateManager.apiKeys` merges data in `applyHomeBootstrap` while `settingsDraftManager.hydrate` correctly replaces normalized against defaults. The fix is to make `apiKeys` also do a replace against `DEFAULT_SERVER_SETTINGS`, and reset it to defaults when `bootstrap.initialQueries.settings` is null instead of retaining stale server data.

The queryFn's merge of the normalized draft over prev is effectively a full overwrite of known keys already, though leftover keys not in defaults could linger — minor issue but worth resolving for consistency. Resetting apiKeys to defaults when settings is null is actually correct behavior for the unauthenticated startup flow, since it clears stale data until the user logs in and the bootstrap reloads with real settings.

But there's a real risk: applyHomeBootstrap fires on every bootstrap, including reconnects of the same server, and a momentary unauthenticated reconnect with settings null would wipe live apiKeys and make projects vanish — a regression versus the current merge behavior. The fix is to only reset on an actual server change, tracked via the bootstrap's runner.runnerInstanceId: if it differs from the last applied id, do a full reset to defaults plus new settings, but if it's the same server, keep the existing merge so a settings-less reconnect doesn't destroy state.

I'm also checking other places that need the same per-server scoping: settingsDraftManager and planningReviewPreferencesManager currently only reset when initialQueries.settings is truthy, so switching to a locked server could leave stale draft data from the previous server — these should reset on runner change too. There's also a GitWorkspaceManager cache keyed only by projectPath in localStorage, which could leak git status across servers with identically-named paths; that's a real but separate bug I'll flag rather than fix now. I'm also noting the omni-collapsed-projects localStorage key as another candidate for similar cross-server leakage.

Now I want to trace through handleAddProject to see if it corrects itself once apiKeys is scoped per server, or whether it still posts a merged blob to the new server that includes stale settings.

      draggedPath,
      targetPath,
      placement,
    });
    if (newProjects === currentProjects) {
      return;
    }

    const updatedKeys = { ...currentKeys, PROJECTS: JSON.stringify(newProjects) };

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:47:16.430Z

I recall that ClaudeAccountAuthManager already scopes per-runner using profileId, which gives a useful precedent. I'm now sketching a fix in home-bootstrap.ts that tracks the applied bootstrap and runner instance IDs so the bootstrap logic can be scoped per-runner similarly.

I'm designing the function to detect when the runner instance has changed and reset the settings draft, planning review preferences, and API keys accordingly, falling back to defaults on a runner switch. Now I'm second-guessing whether the reset should also trigger on the very first application, since the initial state is null and would otherwise just merge over the already-default values, which seems fine either way.

Tracing through switching scenarios confirms the runner identity check works correctly: switching servers resets state to defaults plus new settings, and switching back restores the original correctly, since the runner instance ID comes straight from the server and represents true server identity. I'm also noting that HomeApp remounts per profile and calls the bootstrap function during render, so timing around when this runs matters.

Now I want to confirm that the projects list only comes from apiKeys.PROJECTS parsed in useHomeViewModel and HomeApp, and not from anywhere else like the runs event stream, which appears correctly scoped per-runner already. I should check those specific lines directly and then write a test verifying only the explicit projects source leaks across switches.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:48:23.532Z

All 5 tests pass, but I want to check whether this is really the complete fix. The settings query merges the normalized draft with defaults on runner change, which should be fine, but I'm still concerned about when `applyHomeBootstrap` actually fires during a server switch, since `HomeApp` remounts by `profileId` and calls it during render with the active connection's bootstrap data.

I want to verify OmniApp actually passes the active connection's bootstrap, and check whether it could pass a stale or null bootstrap at the moment of switching, which would let the previous server's projects persist transiently until the new bootstrap arrives -- acceptable, but worth confirming. There are also two separate leaks the subagent flagged that I haven't fixed: the GitWorkspaceManager localStorage cache and the collapsed-projects localStorage entry, both keyed only by path rather than by server, though these would only collide if paths matched across servers -- I'll note them separately. Let me go look at OmniApp now.

function ActiveRunnerApp() {
  useRunnerConnections();
  const connection = useActiveRunnerConnection();
  const connectionSnapshot = connection?.getSnapshot();
  const runtimeApis = connection?.getRuntimeAPIs();
  const bootstrap = connectionSnapshot?.bootstrap;

  if (!connection) {
    return <BootShell />;
  }

  if (!runtimeApis || !bootstrap) {
    return <RunnerRecoveryShell status={connectionSnapshot?.status ?? "connecting"} />;
  }

  return (
    <RuntimeApiProvider apis={runtimeApis}>
      <Suspense fallback={<BootShell />}>
        <HomeApp
          key={connectionSnapshot.profileId}
          bootstrap={bootstrap}
          runnerConnection={connection}
        />
      </Suspense>
    </RuntimeApiProvider>
  );
}

function RunnerRecoveryShell({
  status,
}: {

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:48:38.408Z

Good — HomeApp only renders once the active connection has its own bootstrap from its own connect, so switching correctly triggers applyHomeBootstrap with B's data and there's no window where B's HomeApp shows A's bootstrap. I'm now double-checking the cached snapshot case for a connection that was previously connected and gets switched back to, since its bootstrap id would be the old one.

For an A→B→A sequence, returning to A means A's snapshot still holds its original bootstrap id, but `appliedHomeBootstrapId` currently equals B's id, so the guard correctly lets it proceed. My existing test doesn't quite model this since it generates a fresh id each time, so I should adjust it to reuse A's exact bootstrap object on the switch back to reflect the real cached-snapshot behavior. Extending this to A→B→A→B, the guard only blocks re-applying the immediately previous id, which is the intended behavior.

Reusing A's stale snapshot does mean stale settings temporarily, but the settings query refetches after a short staleTime, so this is acceptable pre-existing behavior. I'll update the test accordingly.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T14:52:25.356Z

That test is confirmed flaky, unrelated to the interface code changes. I'll run typecheck, lint, and build, clean up the temp file, and then reconsider whether there are other places projects need to be scoped per server — beyond the apiKeys fix, I'm now looking at GitWorkspaceManager's localStorage cache, which is keyed only by projectPath and could collide across servers with identical paths.

The collapsed-projects state and the merged apiKeys blob are separate but related caching concerns tied to path-only keys. Since the user's actual complaint is that a new server shows the old server's projects list — which the apiKeys fix already resolves — I'll flag the cache/collision issues rather than expand scope unprompted. I also want to double check that HomeApp actually remounts with the new server's bootstrap and that the applied-bootstrap-id guard correctly distinguishes servers, since if that guard failed, the new server's PROJECTS could still get overridden by the old one's cached settings.

Verifying that each connection maintains its own isolated settings query cache confirms the locked server won't fetch or leak stale apiKeys, and that re-running bootstrap application after unlock correctly merges the new server's actual settings over the reset defaults. That closes the loop, so now it's time to run typecheck, lint, and build to confirm everything holds together.

## Claude · 2026-09-05T14:53:01.001Z

Fixed. The projects list was never per-server.

**Root cause** — `src/interface/home/home-bootstrap.ts:36` merged each server's settings over whatever was already in the global `homeUiStateManager.apiKeys`:

```ts
apiKeys: { ...current.apiKeys, ...settingsValues },
```

A server only reports settings keys it actually has rows for (`src/server/settings/read.ts:38`). Your new server had never saved a `PROJECTS` row, so `settingsValues.PROJECTS` was absent and the old server's value survived the merge. `homeUiStateManager` is a module-level singleton, so it outlives the `HomeApp` remount that a server switch triggers. The whole sidebar derives from that one string (`useHomeViewModel.ts:102`, `HomeApp.tsx:490`).

A locked server hit it harder: when auth is pending, `initialQueries.settings` is `null` entirely (`src/runtime/bootstrap.ts:61`), so hydration was skipped and the merge kept every old value.

This was leaking more than projects. Every setting the new server hadn't saved read as the old server's, and `handleAddProject` posts the whole merged blob back (`useConversationActions.ts:99`), so adding a project on server B could write server A's settings into B's database.

**The fix** tracks `bootstrap.runner.runnerInstanceId` across applications. Arriving from a different server replaces settings against `DEFAULT_SERVER_SETTINGS` instead of merging; reconnecting to the same server still merges, so a bootstrap that arrives without settings can't blank out live state. `settingsDraftManager` and `planningReviewPreferencesManager` now also reset on a server change rather than only when settings are present.

**Verification** — `tests/app/home-bootstrap-runner-scope.test.ts`, five cases, all observed failing first (three failed against the old code with exactly your symptom: `expected '["/work/alpha"]' to be '[]'`). Covers a new server with its own projects, one with none, one still locked, a same-server reconnect keeping live state, and switching back via the cached bootstrap object. Lint clean, interface builds.

Full suite: 3115 passed, 14 failed. I ran every failing file against a pristine copy of HEAD and each fails there identically, including the `tests/app/conversation-transcript-retention.test.ts` typecheck error from earlier. The one exception was `tests/api/conversation-messages-route.test.ts`, which passed on HEAD — it passes twice on re-run here and imports nothing from the interface, so it's a timing flake.

Two smaller per-server leaks I found but did not touch, since they only bite when two servers have identically-named project paths: `GitWorkspaceManager`'s localStorage cache (`omni-git-workspace-cache:v1`) is keyed by project path alone, and `omni-collapsed-projects` stores collapsed state globally. Say the word and I'll scope both by runner instance.
